They’ll call my operator number and say things like I’m dangerous, or that security is coming for me. I don’t think I’ve noticed that exactly. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel as if someone or something is controlling your thoughts or putting thoughts into your mind?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know if I’d say controlling my thoughts exactly. I don’t want to overstate it. But it does feel like the routing system at the warehouse is… sort of marking what I do, tracking my moves, and sending messages through the scanners to steer things against me.\n\nIt’s like it’s trying to push me out because I’m one of the old hands there. I know that probably sounds strange, but that’s honestly how it feels to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself becoming much less interested in seeing people or doing things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have, a bit. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but things I used to like don’t really grab me anymore.\n\nFishing used to settle me down, and choir practice too. I’d mess around with wood in the garage, little projects, and that felt good. Now I can look right at my rods or my tools and just… not feel any pull to pick them up. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well… I’m probably making it sound bigger than it is, doctor, but I’ve been having trouble at work. The routing system at the warehouse, the one we use with the scanners, it doesn’t feel like it’s just a computer anymore. It’s like it’s been tracking my moves and sending little messages through the scanners… kind of pushing me out because I’m one of the old hands there. I know that may not sound right, but that’s what brought me in.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling that the scanner system is tracking you and sending you messages?\n\nPatient: I guess… it’s been longer than I’d like to admit. Maybe about a year and eight months now, with the scanners feeling like they’re sending me messages and the voices tied up with it. I know that sounds strange, but that’s about how long it’s been.\n\nDoctor: When you mention voices tied up with the scanners, do you actually hear a voice speaking to you?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but yes, I do hear it. Even when I’m just sitting in my garage and everything’s shut off, I can hear these dispatch voices clear as day. They’ll call my operator number and say things like I’m dangerous, or that security is coming for me. I know that probably sounds bad… I’m just trying to answer you straight.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any major medical or neurological illness around the time this started?\n\nPatient: Not that I can think of, no. I mean, I’m not a doctor, but I don’t remember any big illness or anything neurological happening right around then. Nothing that stood out to me, anyway. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed your thoughts getting jumbled or hard to keep in order when you speak?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not sure. I don’t think I’ve noticed that exactly. I mean, sometimes I have to slow myself down so I explain things right, but I don’t know if that’s my thoughts being jumbled or just me trying not to sound foolish. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel as if someone or something is controlling your thoughts or putting thoughts into your mind?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know if I’d say controlling my thoughts exactly. I don’t want to overstate it. But it does feel like the routing system at the warehouse is… sort of marking what I do, tracking my moves, and sending messages through the scanners to steer things against me.\n\nIt’s like it’s trying to push me out because I’m one of the old hands there. I know that probably sounds strange, but that’s honestly how it feels to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself becoming much less interested in seeing people or doing things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have, a bit. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but things I used to like don’t really grab me anymore.\n\nFishing used to settle me down, and choir practice too. I’d mess around with wood in the garage, little projects, and that felt good. Now I can look right at my rods or my tools and just… not feel any pull to pick them up. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: During this time, have you had a period of feeling severely depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but it’s been hanging over me most days for… about two years now. Maybe two years and a month, something like that.\n\nIt started not too long after that schedule change at work started wearing me down, and it just kind of stayed there. Is that the kind of time period you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: When the voices happen, are you feeling depressed at those times?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, I think they were. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m blaming everything on that, but when I was at my lowest—barely eating, not going to choir, feeling like I’d let everybody down—the scanner messages and the dispatch voices were happening right along with it.\n\nSo… yes, they weren’t separate from the depressed times, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have the voices ever happened during a time when you were not feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: I… yeah, I think there was. Not as bad as the lowest times, but there was a stretch where my mood had eased up some. I was eating a little better, talking a little more.\n\nBut even then, for several weeks, I’d still hear dispatch calling my number, and I still had it in my head that the routing system was tracking me. I know that probably sounds strange… but that did happen. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you felt emotionally flat or unable to show feelings, even when your mood was not depressed?\n\nPatient: I guess… yes, maybe. I don’t mean to be difficult about it. My wife tells me my face has gone kind of flat, like she can’t read me anymore, and that I barely answer her sometimes.\n\nI don’t always notice it myself. Most days I’ll just sit at the kitchen table looking at my coffee instead of getting up and starting whatever needs doing. I know that probably sounds bad… but yes, even when I’m not feeling quite as depressed, that flatness is still there some.\n\nDoctor: Besides the scanner system, do you believe any other people or things are trying to harm, track, or send messages to you?\n\nPatient: Not… not people exactly. I don’t want to sound paranoid or like I’m accusing coworkers. It’s more the routing system at the warehouse.\n\nTo me it doesn’t feel like just a computer anymore. It feels like it’s marking where I go, what I do, and then the scanner messages kind of… steer things, like they’re pushing me out because I’m one of the old hands there. I know that may not be the right way to explain it, but that’s how it’s been feeling to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that other people can hear or know your thoughts, as if your thoughts are being broadcast?\n\nPatient: Not exactly like everybody can hear my thoughts, no… I don’t think so.\n\nIt’s more that the routing system at the warehouse seems to know what I’m doing, where I’m moving, and then the scanner messages line up with it. Like it’s marking me, sort of… pushing me out because I’ve been there a long time. I know that sounds odd, and I’m not trying to make it bigger than it is, but that’s the part that feels real to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that thoughts were being put into your mind by something outside you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say thoughts are being put in my mind, exactly. I’m not trying to make it sound stranger than it is.\n\nIt’s more… I’ll be at work and the routing system doesn’t feel like just a computer anymore. It feels like it knows where I’m going, like it’s marking my moves, and then the scanners send messages that seem aimed at me. Like they’re trying to push me out because I’ve been there so long, one of the old hands.\n\nI know that may not be the answer you’re looking for, but that’s the closest way I can explain it.\n\nDoctor: When you’re not feeling depressed, do you still find it hard to get yourself to start everyday things, even simple ones?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I try not to make too much of it. I’m not trying to exaggerate it. That’s just what it felt like.\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a time when the voices and scanner messages were happening but you were not feeling depressed at all?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think there was a time like that.
